FILE: lotus-core/src/calc/parser.rs
================================================================================
//! Recursive-descent Pratt parser for spreadsheet formula expressions.

#[cfg(not(feature = "std"))]
use alloc::{boxed::Box, string::String, vec::Vec};

use super::{
    ast::{BinaryOp, Expr, UnaryOp},
    lexer::{Lexer, Token},
};
use crate::model::Coord;

#[derive(Debug, PartialEq)]
pub enum ParseError {
    UnexpectedToken(String),
    UnexpectedEof,
    InvalidReference(String),
}

pub struct Parser {
    tokens: Vec<Token>,
    pos: usize,
}

impl Parser {
    pub fn new(tokens: Vec<Token>) -> Self {
        Self { tokens, pos: 0 }
    }

    pub fn parse_str(input: &str) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
        let tokens = Lexer::new(input).tokenize();
        let mut parser = Self::new(tokens);
        let expr = parser.parse_expr(0)?;
        if !parser.is_eof() {
            return Err(ParseError::UnexpectedToken("Trailing tokens".into()));
        }
        Ok(expr)
    }

    fn peek(&self) -> Option<&Token> {
        self.tokens.get(self.pos)
    }

    fn advance(&mut self) -> Option<Token> {
        if self.pos < self.tokens.len() {
            let tok = self.tokens[self.pos].clone();
            self.pos += 1;
            Some(tok)
        } else {
            None
        }
    }

    fn is_eof(&self) -> bool {
        self.pos >= self.tokens.len()
    }

    fn parse_expr(&mut self, min_bp: u8) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
        let mut lhs = self.parse_primary()?;

        loop {
            let op = match self.peek() {
                Some(Token::Plus) => BinaryOp::Add,
                Some(Token::Minus) => BinaryOp::Sub,
                Some(Token::Star) => BinaryOp::Mul,
                Some(Token::Slash) => BinaryOp::Div,
                Some(Token::Percent) => BinaryOp::Mod,
                Some(Token::Caret) => BinaryOp::Pow,
                Some(Token::Ampersand) => BinaryOp::Concat,
                Some(Token::Eq) => BinaryOp::Eq,
                Some(Token::NotEq) => BinaryOp::NotEq,
                Some(Token::Lt) => BinaryOp::Lt,
                Some(Token::LtEq) => BinaryOp::LtEq,
                Some(Token::Gt) => BinaryOp::Gt,
                Some(Token::GtEq) => BinaryOp::GtEq,
                _ => break,
            };

            let (l_bp, r_bp) = infix_binding_power(op);
            if l_bp < min_bp {
                break;
            }

            self.advance(); // consume operator
            let rhs = self.parse_expr(r_bp)?;
            lhs = Expr::Binary {
                op,
                left: Box::new(lhs),
                right: Box::new(rhs),
            };
        }

        Ok(lhs)
    }

    fn parse_primary(&mut self) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
        let tok = self.advance().ok_or(ParseError::UnexpectedEof)?;

        match tok {
            Token::Number(n) => Ok(Expr::Number(n)),
            Token::StringLit(s) => Ok(Expr::Text(s)),
            Token::Minus => {
                let expr = self.parse_expr(10)?;
                Ok(Expr::Unary {
                    op: UnaryOp::Neg,
                    expr: Box::new(expr),
                })
            }
            Token::LParen => {
                let inner = self.parse_expr(0)?;
                match self.advance() {
                    Some(Token::RParen) => Ok(inner),
                    _ => Err(ParseError::UnexpectedToken("Expected ')'".into())),
                }
            }
            Token::Ident(name) => {
                let upper = name.to_ascii_uppercase();

                // Boolean literals
                if upper == "TRUE" {
                    return Ok(Expr::Boolean(true));
                }
                if upper == "FALSE" {
                    return Ok(Expr::Boolean(false));
                }

                // Function call: IDENT '(' args ')'
                if self.peek() == Some(&Token::LParen) {
                    self.advance(); // consume '('
                    let mut args = Vec::new();
                    if self.peek() != Some(&Token::RParen) {
                        loop {
                            args.push(self.parse_expr(0)?);
                            match self.peek() {
                                Some(Token::Comma) => {
                                    self.advance();
                                }
                                Some(Token::RParen) => break,
                                _ => return Err(ParseError::UnexpectedToken("Expected ',' or ')' in argument list".into())),
                            }
                        }
                    }
                    self.advance(); // consume ')'
                    return Ok(Expr::FunctionCall { name: upper, args });
                }

                // Range reference: IDENT ':' IDENT (e.g. A1:B10)
                if self.peek() == Some(&Token::Colon) {
                    self.advance(); // consume ':'
                    let right_tok = self.advance().ok_or(ParseError::UnexpectedEof)?;
                    if let Token::Ident(right_name) = right_tok {
                        let c1 = Coord::parse_a1(&strip_dollar(&name))
                            .ok_or_else(|| ParseError::InvalidReference(name.clone()))?;
                        let c2 = Coord::parse_a1(&strip_dollar(&right_name))
                            .ok_or_else(|| ParseError::InvalidReference(right_name.clone()))?;

                        let top_left = Coord::new(c1.col.min(c2.col), c1.row.min(c2.row));
                        let bottom_right = Coord::new(c1.col.max(c2.col), c1.row.max(c2.row));

                        return Ok(Expr::RangeRef { top_left, bottom_right });
                    } else {
                        return Err(ParseError::UnexpectedToken("Expected coordinate after ':'".into()));
                    }
                }

                // Single Cell Reference or Named Reference
                let cleaned = strip_dollar(&name);
                if let Some(coord) = Coord::parse_a1(&cleaned) {
                    Ok(Expr::CellRef(coord))
                } else {
                    Ok(Expr::NamedRef(name))
                }
            }
            _ => Err(ParseError::UnexpectedToken("Invalid primary expression".into())),
        }
    }
}

fn strip_dollar(s: &str) -> String {
    s.replace('$', "")
}

fn infix_binding_power(op: BinaryOp) -> (u8, u8) {
    match op {
        BinaryOp::Eq | BinaryOp::NotEq | BinaryOp::Lt | BinaryOp::LtEq | BinaryOp::Gt | BinaryOp::GtEq => (1, 2),
        BinaryOp::Concat => (3, 4),
        BinaryOp::Add | BinaryOp::Sub => (5, 6),
        BinaryOp::Mul | BinaryOp::Div | BinaryOp::Mod => (7, 8),
        BinaryOp::Pow => (10, 9), // Right-associative
    }
}

================================================================================
FILE: lotus-core/src/calc/refs.rs
================================================================================
//! Rewriting cell references inside formula text.
//!
//! Formulas are stored as text (`=A1*B2`), so anything that moves cells
//! (insert or delete rows and columns, fill, paste, sort) has to fix up the
//! references inside them. This works on the text itself and leaves everything
//! else exactly as typed: spacing, string literals, function names and named
//! references are never touched.
//!
//! What counts as a reference follows the lexer: an identifier that parses as
//! A1 (`Coord::parse_a1`) and is not followed by `(`. `A1:B2` is a range.

#[cfg(not(feature = "std"))]
use alloc::{string::String, vec::Vec};

use crate::model::Coord;

pub const MAX_COL: u32 = 16_383;
pub const MAX_ROW: u32 = 1_048_575;

/// What a rewritten reference is: a lone cell, or one end of a range.
/// Deletions use this to shrink a range instead of breaking it.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum RefKind {
    Cell,
    RangeStart,
    RangeEnd,
}

const REF_ERROR: &str = "#REF!";

enum Seg<'a> {
    Lit(&'a str),
    Ref(Coord),
}

fn segments(formula: &str) -> Vec<Seg<'_>> {
    let bytes = formula.as_bytes();
    let mut segs = Vec::new();
    let mut lit_start = 0;
    let mut i = 0;
    let mut in_string = false;

    while i < bytes.len() {
        let b = bytes[i];
        if in_string {
            if b == b'"' {
                in_string = false;
            }
            i += 1;
            continue;
        }
        if b == b'"' {
            in_string = true;
            i += 1;
            continue;
        }
        if b.is_ascii_alphabetic() || b == b'_' {
            let start = i;
            while i < bytes.len() && (bytes[i].is_ascii_alphanumeric() || bytes[i] == b'_' || bytes[i] == b'$') {
                i += 1;
            }
            let mut j = i;
            while j < bytes.len() && bytes[j].is_ascii_whitespace() {
                j += 1;
            }
            let is_call = j < bytes.len() && bytes[j] == b'(';
            if !is_call {
                if let Some(coord) = Coord::parse_a1(&formula[start..i]) {
                    segs.push(Seg::Lit(&formula[lit_start..start]));
                    segs.push(Seg::Ref(coord));
                    lit_start = i;
                }
            }
            continue;
        }
        i += 1;
    }
    segs.push(Seg::Lit(&formula[lit_start..]));
    segs
}

/// Rewrite every cell reference in `formula` through `map`.
///
/// `map` returns the new coordinate, or `None` when the referenced cell no
/// longer exists; that reference becomes `#REF!`. A range whose ends no longer
/// line up (everything it covered was deleted) also becomes `#REF!`.
pub fn rewrite_refs(formula: &str, map: &mut dyn FnMut(Coord, RefKind) -> Option<Coord>) -> String {
    let segs = segments(formula);
    let mut out = String::with_capacity(formula.len() + 8);
    let mut k = 0;

    while k < segs.len() {
        match &segs[k] {
            Seg::Lit(text) => {
                out.push_str(text);
                k += 1;
            }
            Seg::Ref(a) => {
                let range_tail = match (segs.get(k + 1), segs.get(k + 2)) {
                    (Some(Seg::Lit(mid)), Some(Seg::Ref(b))) if mid.trim() == ":" => Some((*mid, *b)),
                    _ => None,
                };
                if let Some((mid, b)) = range_tail {
                    let was_ordered = a.col <= b.col && a.row <= b.row;
                    let new_a = map(*a, RefKind::RangeStart);
                    let new_b = map(b, RefKind::RangeEnd);
                    match (new_a, new_b) {
                        (Some(x), Some(y)) if !was_ordered || (x.col <= y.col && x.row <= y.row) => {
                            out.push_str(&x.to_a1());
                            out.push_str(mid);
                            out.push_str(&y.to_a1());
                        }
                        _ => out.push_str(REF_ERROR),
                    }
                    k += 3;
                } else {
                    match map(*a, RefKind::Cell) {
                        Some(c) => out.push_str(&c.to_a1()),
                        None => out.push_str(REF_ERROR),
                    }
                    k += 1;
                }
            }
        }
    }
    out
}

/// Move every reference by a fixed offset, as copy/paste, fill and sort do.
/// A reference pushed off the sheet becomes `#REF!`.
pub fn offset_refs(formula: &str, d_col: i64, d_row: i64) -> String {
    rewrite_refs(formula, &mut |c, _| {
        let col = i64::from(c.col) + d_col;
        let row = i64::from(c.row) + d_row;
        if (0..=i64::from(MAX_COL)).contains(&col) && (0..=i64::from(MAX_ROW)).contains(&row) {
            Some(Coord::new(col as u32, row as u32))
        } else {
            None
        }
    })
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn offset_moves_every_reference_and_keeps_spacing() {
        assert_eq!(offset_refs("=A1 * B2", 1, 1), "=B2 * C3");
        assert_eq!(offset_refs("=A1+A2", 0, 3), "=A4+A5");
        assert_eq!(offset_refs("=B3-A1", -1, -1), "=A2-#REF!");
    }

    #[test]
    fn offset_moves_both_ends_of_a_range() {
        assert_eq!(offset_refs("=SUM(A1:A5)", 2, 0), "=SUM(C1:C5)");
        assert_eq!(offset_refs("=SUM(A1 : B2)", 0, 1), "=SUM(A2 : B3)");
    }

    #[test]
    fn function_names_strings_and_names_are_left_alone() {
        // LOG10 looks like a cell (column LOG, row 10) but is followed by '('.
        assert_eq!(offset_refs("=LOG10(A1)", 0, 1), "=LOG10(A2)");
        assert_eq!(offset_refs("=\"A1\"&B1", 1, 0), "=\"A1\"&C1");
        assert_eq!(offset_refs("=revenue * tax_rate", 5, 5), "=revenue * tax_rate");
        assert_eq!(offset_refs("=IF(TRUE, A1, 0)", 0, 1), "=IF(TRUE, A2, 0)");
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_reference_pushed_off_the_sheet_becomes_ref_error() {
        assert_eq!(offset_refs("=A1", -1, 0), "=#REF!");
        assert_eq!(offset_refs("=A1+B1", -1, 0), "=#REF!+A1");
        assert_eq!(offset_refs("=SUM(A1:B2)", 0, -1), "=SUM(#REF!)");
    }

    #[test]
    fn rewrite_can_shrink_a_range_by_kind() {
        // Pretend row 5 (zero-based 4) was deleted: the end moves up one.
        let out = rewrite_refs("=SUM(A1:A5)", &mut |c, kind| match (c.row, kind) {
            (4, RefKind::RangeEnd) => Some(Coord::new(c.col, 3)),
            _ => Some(c),
        });
        assert_eq!(out, "=SUM(A1:A4)");
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_inverted_range_is_a_ref_error() {
        let out = rewrite_refs("=SUM(A2:A3)", &mut |c, kind| match kind {
            RefKind::RangeStart => Some(Coord::new(c.col, 5)),
            _ => Some(Coord::new(c.col, 1)),
        });
        assert_eq!(out, "=SUM(#REF!)");
    }

    #[test]
    fn lowercase_and_non_ascii_text_survive() {
        assert_eq!(offset_refs("=a1 & \"é\"", 1, 0), "=B1 & \"é\"");
        assert_eq!(offset_refs("", 1, 1), "");
        assert_eq!(offset_refs("=1+2", 1, 1), "=1+2");
    }
}

//! Embedded Documents, Cross-References, and Live Templates.
//!
//! As specified in Lotus 5-7-11 Section 6:
//! - Documents (Markdown notes, specifications, reports) are first-class workbook objects.
//! - Cell-to-document references allow cells to link to supporting context and section anchors (`doc#fragment`).
//! - Document-to-cell references embed live spreadsheet values (e.g. `{{cell:revenue}}` or `{{cell:A1}}`)
//!   or immutable point-in-time snapshots (`{{snapshot:A1}}`).

#[cfg(not(feature = "std"))]
use alloc::{format, string::String, string::ToString, vec, vec::Vec};

use crate::model::{Cell, Coord, Sheet, Workbook};

/// Representation of a parsed Markdown section heading.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct SectionHeading {
    pub level: usize,
    pub title: String,
    pub anchor: String,
    pub line_index: usize,
}

/// Convert a heading title into a URL/anchor slug:
/// - Lowercase ASCII
/// - Alphanumerics retained
/// - Spaces, underscores, slashes, and hyphens collapsed into single dashes
/// - Leading and trailing dashes trimmed
pub fn heading_to_slug(title: &str) -> String {
    let mut slug = String::new();
    let mut prev_dash = false;
    for ch in title.chars() {
        if ch.is_ascii_alphanumeric() {
            slug.push(ch.to_ascii_lowercase());
            prev_dash = false;
        } else if ch == ' ' || ch == '-' || ch == '_' || ch == '/' {
            if !prev_dash && !slug.is_empty() {
                slug.push('-');
                prev_dash = true;
            }
        }
    }
    if slug.ends_with('-') {
        slug.pop();
    }
    slug
}

/// Parse a line to check if it represents a Markdown heading (1 to 6 `#` characters followed by space/tab/EOL).
pub fn parse_heading_line(line: &str) -> Option<(usize, String)> {
    let trimmed = line.trim_start();
    if !trimmed.starts_with('#') {
        return None;
    }

    let mut level = 0;
    let chars: Vec<char> = trimmed.chars().collect();
    while level < chars.len() && chars[level] == '#' && level < 6 {
        level += 1;
    }

    if level == 0 {
        return None;
    }

    if level < chars.len() && chars[level] != ' ' && chars[level] != '\t' {
        return None;
    }

    let title: String = chars[level..].iter().collect();
    Some((level, title.trim().to_string()))
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct Document {
    pub id: String,
    pub title: String,
    pub content_type: String, // e.g. "text/markdown"
    pub body: String,
}

impl Document {
    pub fn new(id: impl Into<String>, title: impl Into<String>, body: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        Self {
            id: id.into(),
            title: title.into(),
            content_type: "text/markdown".into(),
            body: body.into(),
        }
    }

    /// Renders the document body by resolving live cell references:
    /// - `{{cell:A1}}` or `{{cell:name}}` resolves against the current live sheet state.
    pub fn render_live(&self, sheet: &Sheet, workbook: Option<&Workbook>) -> String {
        render_template_str(&self.body, sheet, workbook)
    }

    /// Return explicit `{{cell:target}}` references in document order.
    pub fn referenced_cells(&self) -> Vec<String> {
        let mut references = Vec::new();
        let mut remaining = self.body.as_str();
        while let Some(start) = remaining.find("{{") {
            remaining = &remaining[start + 2..];
            let Some(end) = remaining.find("}}") else { break };
            let tag = remaining[..end].trim();
            if let Some(target) = tag.strip_prefix("cell:") {
                let target = target.trim();
                if !target.is_empty() {
                    references.push(target.to_string());
                }
            }
            remaining = &remaining[end + 2..];
        }
        references
    }

    /// Whether this document explicitly references the supplied cell by A1 coordinate or name.
    pub fn references_cell(&self, cell: &Cell, sheet_id: &str, default_sheet_id: &str) -> bool {
        self.referenced_cells().iter().any(|target| {
            let (target_sheet_id, target_cell) = target.split_once('!')
                .unwrap_or((default_sheet_id, target.as_str()));
            target_sheet_id == sheet_id
                && (Coord::parse_a1(target_cell).is_some_and(|coord| coord == cell.coord)
                    || cell.name.as_deref() == Some(target_cell))
        })
    }

    /// Extract all Markdown section headings from the document body.
    pub fn headings(&self) -> Vec<SectionHeading> {
        let mut headings = Vec::new();
        for (line_index, line) in self.body.lines().enumerate() {
            if let Some((level, title)) = parse_heading_line(line) {
                let anchor = heading_to_slug(&title);
                headings.push(SectionHeading {
                    level,
                    title,
                    anchor,
                    line_index,
                });
            }
        }
        headings
    }

    /// Find a section heading matching a fragment (by canonical slug, case-insensitive anchor, or title).
    pub fn find_section(&self, fragment: &str) -> Option<SectionHeading> {
        let clean_frag = fragment.strip_prefix('#').unwrap_or(fragment).trim();
        if clean_frag.is_empty() {
            return None;
        }
        let frag_slug = heading_to_slug(clean_frag);

        self.headings().into_iter().find(|h| {
            h.anchor == clean_frag.to_ascii_lowercase()
                || h.anchor == frag_slug
                || h.title.eq_ignore_ascii_case(clean_frag)
        })
    }

    /// Check whether a section anchor/heading exists in this document.
    pub fn has_section(&self, fragment: &str) -> bool {
        self.find_section(fragment).is_some()
    }

    /// Extract the unrendered body text of a specific section (from the matching heading
    /// up to the next heading of equal or higher level, or end of document).
    pub fn section_content(&self, fragment: &str) -> Option<String> {
        let target = self.find_section(fragment)?;
        let lines: Vec<&str> = self.body.lines().collect();
        let mut section_lines = Vec::new();

        for (idx, line) in lines.iter().enumerate().skip(target.line_index) {
            if idx > target.line_index {
                if let Some((level, _)) = parse_heading_line(line) {
                    if level <= target.level {
                        break;
                    }
                }
            }
            section_lines.push(*line);
        }

        Some(section_lines.join("\n"))
    }

    /// Render a specific section of the document with live cell references resolved.
    pub fn render_section(&self, fragment: &str, sheet: &Sheet, workbook: Option<&Workbook>) -> Option<String> {
        let content = self.section_content(fragment)?;
        Some(render_template_str(&content, sheet, workbook))
    }
}

/// Render live cell templates in arbitrary text against the given sheet and workbook.
pub fn render_template_str(text: &str, sheet: &Sheet, workbook: Option<&Workbook>) -> String {
    let mut out = String::new();
    let chars: Vec<char> = text.chars().collect();
    let mut i = 0;

    while i < chars.len() {
        if i + 1 < chars.len() && chars[i] == '{' && chars[i + 1] == '{' {
            let start = i + 2;
            let mut end = start;
            while end + 1 < chars.len() && !(chars[end] == '}' && chars[end + 1] == '}') {
                end += 1;
            }

            if end + 1 < chars.len() {
                let tag: String = chars[start..end].iter().collect();
                let resolved = resolve_tag(tag.trim(), sheet, workbook);
                out.push_str(&resolved);
                i = end + 2;
                continue;
            }
        }
        out.push(chars[i]);
        i += 1;
    }

    out
}

fn resolve_tag(tag: &str, sheet: &Sheet, workbook: Option<&Workbook>) -> String {
    if let Some(target) = tag.strip_prefix("cell:") {
        let target = target.trim();
        let (target_sheet, target) = if let Some((sheet_id, cell_target)) = target.split_once('!') {
            let Some(target_sheet) = workbook.and_then(|workbook| workbook.get_sheet(sheet_id.trim())) else {
                return format!("<unknown-sheet:{}>", sheet_id.trim());
            };
            (target_sheet, cell_target.trim())
        } else {
            (sheet, target)
        };
        // Try coordinate first
        let coord = if let Some(c) = Coord::parse_a1(target) {
            Some(c)
        } else {
            // Try cell name
            target_sheet.cells.values().find(|c| c.name.as_deref() == Some(target)).map(|c| c.coord)
        };

        if let Some(c) = coord {
            if let Some(cell) = target_sheet.get_cell(c) {
                match &cell.value {
                    crate::model::CellValue::Number(n) => format!("{:.2}", n),
                    crate::model::CellValue::Text(t) => t.clone(),
                    crate::model::CellValue::Boolean(b) => (if *b { "TRUE" } else { "FALSE" }).into(),
                    crate::model::CellValue::Formula { cached_num, cached_text, cached_bool, expr } => {
                        if let Some(n) = cached_num {
                            format!("{:.2}", n)
                        } else if let Some(value) = cached_bool {
                            (if *value { "TRUE" } else { "FALSE" }).into()
                        } else if let Some(ref t) = cached_text {
                            t.clone()
                        } else {
                            format!("={}", expr)
                        }
                    }
                    _ => "<unsupported>".into(),
                }
            } else {
                "<empty>".into()
            }
        } else {
            format!("<unknown:{}>", target)
        }
    } else {
        format!("{{{{{}}}}}", tag)
    }
}

/// Status of an outgoing document reference (including optional section fragment).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum DocumentRefStatus {
    /// Document exists and no section fragment was specified.
    Valid,
    /// Document exists and the section fragment was resolved to a valid heading.
    ValidWithSection(String),
    /// The referenced document does not exist in the workbook.
    UnresolvedDocument,
    /// The document exists, but the section fragment could not be found.
    UnresolvedSection(String),
}

/// Broken outgoing document link from a worksheet cell.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct BrokenDocRef {
    pub sheet_id: String,
    pub coord: Coord,
    pub cell_name: Option<String>,
    pub target: String,
    pub reason: String,
}

/// Broken section anchor in an outgoing document link.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct BrokenSectionRef {
    pub sheet_id: String,
    pub coord: Coord,
    pub cell_name: Option<String>,
    pub document_id: String,
    pub section_anchor: String,
    pub target: String,
}

/// Broken cell reference embedded within a document template (`{{cell:...}}`).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct BrokenDocCellRef {
    pub document_id: String,
    pub target: String,
    pub reason: String,
}

/// Dangling formula reference pointing to an empty or non-existent cell.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct DanglingFormulaRef {
    pub sheet_id: String,
    pub coord: Coord,
    pub formula_name: Option<String>,
    pub target: String,
}

/// Comprehensive report of orphaned references and missing links across the workbook.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Default)]
pub struct OrphanReport {
    pub broken_document_refs: Vec<BrokenDocRef>,
    pub broken_section_refs: Vec<BrokenSectionRef>,
    pub broken_document_cell_refs: Vec<BrokenDocCellRef>,
    pub unreferenced_documents: Vec<String>,
    pub dangling_formula_refs: Vec<DanglingFormulaRef>,
}

impl OrphanReport {
    pub fn is_clean(&self) -> bool {
        self.broken_document_refs.is_empty()
            && self.broken_section_refs.is_empty()
            && self.broken_document_cell_refs.is_empty()
            && self.unreferenced_documents.is_empty()
            && self.dangling_formula_refs.is_empty()
    }

    pub fn total_issues(&self) -> usize {
        self.broken_document_refs.len()
            + self.broken_section_refs.len()
            + self.broken_document_cell_refs.len()
            + self.unreferenced_documents.len()
            + self.dangling_formula_refs.len()
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::model::{Cell, Coord};

    #[test]
    fn test_embedded_document_live_resolution() {
        let mut sheet = Sheet::new("main", "Main");
        sheet.set_cell(Cell::number(Coord::new(0, 0), 12500.5).with_name("revenue"));
        sheet.set_cell(Cell::number(Coord::new(0, 1), 0.25).with_name("tax_rate"));
        sheet.set_cell(Cell::number(Coord::new(0, 2), 3125.125).with_name("tax_amount"));

        let doc_body = r#"# Executive Summary
The model currently projects total revenue of ${{cell:revenue}} (cell A1).
With a declared tax rate of {{cell:A2}}, the estimated tax liability is ${{cell:tax_amount}}.
"#;

        let doc = Document::new("exec-summary", "Executive Summary", doc_body);
        let rendered = doc.render_live(&sheet, None);

        assert!(rendered.contains("total revenue of $12500.50 (cell A1)"));
        assert!(rendered.contains("tax rate of 0.25"));
        assert!(rendered.contains("estimated tax liability is $3125.12"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn document_cell_references_are_exposed_as_explicit_graph_edges() {
        let mut sheet = Sheet::new("main", "Main");
        let cell = Cell::number(Coord::new(0, 0), 42.0)
            .with_name("answer")
            .with_document_refs(vec!["analysis#results".into()]);
        sheet.set_cell(cell);
        let document = Document::new("analysis", "Analysis", "{{cell:answer}} / {{cell:A1}} / ordinary prose");
        let workbook = Workbook::new("w", "w")
            .with_sheet(sheet)
            .with_document(document);

        let cell = workbook.get_sheet("main").unwrap().get_cell(Coord::new(0, 0)).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(cell.document_refs, vec!["analysis#results"]);
        assert_eq!(workbook.documents_linked_from_cell("main", Coord::new(0, 0)).len(), 1);
        assert_eq!(workbook.documents_referencing_cell("main", Coord::new(0, 0)).len(), 1);
        assert_eq!(workbook.documents[0].referenced_cells(), vec!["answer", "A1"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn qualified_document_cell_references_resolve_across_sheets() {
        let main = Sheet::new("main", "Main");
        let mut detail = Sheet::new("detail", "Details");
        detail.set_cell(Cell::number(Coord::new(0, 0), 7.0).with_name("detail_value"));
        let document = Document::new("analysis", "Analysis", "Detail: {{cell:detail!detail_value}}");
        let workbook = Workbook::new("w", "w")
            .with_sheet(main)
            .with_sheet(detail)
            .with_document(document);

        let rendered = workbook.documents[0].render_live(workbook.primary_sheet().unwrap(), Some(&workbook));
        assert_eq!(rendered, "Detail: 7.00");
        assert_eq!(workbook.documents_referencing_cell("detail", Coord::new(0, 0)).len(), 1);
        assert!(workbook.documents_referencing_cell("main", Coord::new(0, 0)).is_empty());
    }

    #[test]
    fn markdown_headings_are_parsed_and_slugified() {
        let body = r#"# Main Title
Introductory text.

## 1. Inputs & Parameters
Some parameters.

### Detailed Rate Table
Details here.

## Summary
The end.
"#;
        let doc = Document::new("test", "Test Doc", body);
        let headings = doc.headings();
        assert_eq!(headings.len(), 4);
        assert_eq!(headings[0].title, "Main Title");
        assert_eq!(headings[0].anchor, "main-title");
        assert_eq!(headings[0].level, 1);

        assert_eq!(headings[1].title, "1. Inputs & Parameters");
        assert_eq!(headings[1].anchor, "1-inputs-parameters");
        assert_eq!(headings[1].level, 2);

        assert_eq!(headings[2].title, "Detailed Rate Table");
        assert_eq!(headings[2].anchor, "detailed-rate-table");
        assert_eq!(headings[2].level, 3);

        assert_eq!(headings[3].title, "Summary");
        assert_eq!(headings[3].anchor, "summary");
        assert_eq!(headings[3].level, 2);
    }

    #[test]
    fn markdown_section_content_and_live_rendering() {
        let mut sheet = Sheet::new("main", "Main");
        sheet.set_cell(Cell::number(Coord::new(0, 0), 5000.0).with_name("revenue"));
        sheet.set_cell(Cell::number(Coord::new(0, 1), 0.10).with_name("rate"));

        let body = r#"# Overview
General notes.

## Section Alpha
Revenue is ${{cell:revenue}}.

## Section Beta
Rate is {{cell:rate}}.

# Conclusion
Final thoughts.
"#;
        let doc = Document::new("report", "Report", body);

        // Find by exact anchor
        let heading_alpha = doc.find_section("section-alpha").expect("section alpha found");
        assert_eq!(heading_alpha.title, "Section Alpha");

        // Find with leading #
        assert!(doc.has_section("#section-beta"));

        // Find by title case-insensitive
        assert!(doc.has_section("section beta"));

        // Extract section content
        let alpha_content = doc.section_content("section-alpha").unwrap();
        assert!(alpha_content.contains("Revenue is ${{cell:revenue}}."));
        assert!(!alpha_content.contains("Section Beta"));

        // Render live section
        let rendered_alpha = doc.render_section("section-alpha", &sheet, None).unwrap();
        assert!(rendered_alpha.contains("Revenue is $5000.00."));

        // Non-existent section
        assert!(doc.find_section("missing-section").is_none());
        assert!(doc.render_section("missing-section", &sheet, None).is_none());
    }
}

FILE: lotus-core/src/history/mod.rs

//! Z-Axis Historical Layers, Provenance, and Tombstone Overlays.
//!
//! As specified in Lotus 5-7-11 Section 3.6:
//! - The Z axis represents discrete historical layers in sequence (a stack of thin sheets).
//! - History is represented as overlays, avoiding duplicate full-workbook snapshots.
//! - Moving along the Z axis selects earlier or later historical states.
//! - Explicit tombstones track deletions so historical restoration remains unambiguous.

#[cfg(not(feature = "std"))]
use alloc::{collections::BTreeMap, format, string::String, vec::Vec};
#[cfg(feature = "std")]
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

use crate::model::{Cell, Coord, Sheet};

/// A mutation event recording historical provenance along the Z axis.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct HistoricalRecord {
    pub z: u32,
    pub coord: Coord,
    pub cell: Option<Cell>, // None represents an explicit Tombstone (deletion)
}

/// Manages multi-layered Z-axis history and provenance overlays.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct HistoryEngine {
    pub current_z: u32,
    pub max_z: u32,
    pub layers: BTreeMap<u32, Vec<HistoricalRecord>>,
}

impl HistoryEngine {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            current_z: 0,
            max_z: 0,
            layers: BTreeMap::new(),
        }
    }

    /// Record a snapshot layer of changed cells and deletions.
    pub fn record_layer(&mut self, changes: Vec<(Coord, Option<Cell>)>) -> Result<u32, &'static str> {
        let z = self.max_z.checked_add(1).ok_or("History Z axis is exhausted")?;
        self.insert_layer(z, changes)?;
        Ok(z)
    }

    /// Insert a serialized layer while preserving its Z value.
    /// Layers must arrive consecutively, and each cell may appear once per layer.
    pub fn insert_layer(
        &mut self,
        z: u32,
        changes: Vec<(Coord, Option<Cell>)>,
    ) -> Result<(), &'static str> {
        let expected_z = self.max_z.checked_add(1).ok_or("History Z axis is exhausted")?;
        if z != expected_z {
            return Err("History layers must have consecutive Z values starting at 1");
        }
        for (i, (coord, cell)) in changes.iter().enumerate() {
            if cell.as_ref().is_some_and(|value| value.coord != *coord) {
                return Err("History cell coordinate does not match its record coordinate");
            }
            if changes[..i].iter().any(|(prior, _)| prior == coord) {
                return Err("A cell may appear only once in a history layer");
            }
        }
        let records = changes
            .into_iter()
            .map(|(coord, cell)| HistoricalRecord { z, coord, cell })
            .collect();
        self.layers.insert(z, records);
        self.max_z = z;
        self.current_z = z;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Construct a composite `Sheet` view at a specific discrete Z layer.
    ///
    /// Applies overlays sequentially from layer 0 up to `target_z`:
    /// - Unchanged cells persist.
    /// - Changed cells expose their state at or before `target_z`.
    /// - Deletions (tombstones) suppress cell visibility at and after that layer.
    pub fn composite_at_z(&self, base_sheet: &Sheet, target_z: u32) -> Sheet {
        let mut composite_sheet = Sheet::new(&base_sheet.id, format!("{} (Z={})", base_sheet.name, target_z));

        // Start with base sheet cells (layer 0)
        for (_coord, cell) in &base_sheet.cells {
            composite_sheet.set_cell(cell.clone());
        }

        // Apply layers 1..=target_z in chronological order
        for (z, records) in &self.layers {
            if *z > target_z {
                break;
            }
            for rec in records {
                match &rec.cell {
                    Some(updated_cell) => {
                        composite_sheet.set_cell(updated_cell.clone());
                    }
                    None => {
                        // Tombstone: explicitly remove cell from composite view
                        composite_sheet.cells.remove(&rec.coord);
                    }
                }
            }
        }

        composite_sheet
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::model::CellValue;

    #[test]
    fn test_z_axis_history_and_tombstones() {
        let mut base_sheet = Sheet::new("main", "Main");
        // Layer 0: A1 = 10, A2 = 20
        base_sheet.set_cell(Cell::number(Coord::new(0, 0), 10.0));
        base_sheet.set_cell(Cell::number(Coord::new(0, 1), 20.0));

        let mut history = HistoryEngine::new();

        // Layer 1: Update A1 = 50, Add A3 = 30
        let z1 = history.record_layer(vec![
            (Coord::new(0, 0), Some(Cell::number(Coord::new(0, 0), 50.0))),
            (Coord::new(0, 2), Some(Cell::number(Coord::new(0, 2), 30.0))),
        ]).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(z1, 1);

        // Layer 2: Tombstone (delete) A2, Update A3 = 99
        let z2 = history.record_layer(vec![
            (Coord::new(0, 1), None), // Tombstone on A2
            (Coord::new(0, 2), Some(Cell::number(Coord::new(0, 2), 99.0))),
        ]).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(z2, 2);

        // View at Layer 0 (Original state)
        let view_z0 = history.composite_at_z(&base_sheet, 0);
        assert_eq!(view_z0.cells.len(), 2);
        assert_eq!(view_z0.get_cell(Coord::new(0, 0)).unwrap().value, CellValue::Number(10.0));
        assert_eq!(view_z0.get_cell(Coord::new(0, 1)).unwrap().value, CellValue::Number(20.0));
        assert!(view_z0.get_cell(Coord::new(0, 2)).is_none());

        // View at Layer 1 (A1 modified, A3 added, A2 still present)
        let view_z1 = history.composite_at_z(&base_sheet, 1);
        assert_eq!(view_z1.cells.len(), 3);
        assert_eq!(view_z1.get_cell(Coord::new(0, 0)).unwrap().value, CellValue::Number(50.0));
        assert_eq!(view_z1.get_cell(Coord::new(0, 1)).unwrap().value, CellValue::Number(20.0));
        assert_eq!(view_z1.get_cell(Coord::new(0, 2)).unwrap().value, CellValue::Number(30.0));

        // View at Layer 2 (A2 deleted by tombstone, A3 updated to 99)
        let view_z2 = history.composite_at_z(&base_sheet, 2);
        assert_eq!(view_z2.cells.len(), 2);
        assert_eq!(view_z2.get_cell(Coord::new(0, 0)).unwrap().value, CellValue::Number(50.0));
        assert!(view_z2.get_cell(Coord::new(0, 1)).is_none(), "A2 should be tombstoned");
        assert_eq!(view_z2.get_cell(Coord::new(0, 2)).unwrap().value, CellValue::Number(99.0));
    }
}

================================================================================
FILE: lotus-core/src/memory/arena.rs

//! Autonomous memory space management for OS-agnostic and bare-metal execution.
//!
//! Provides a self-contained bump and arena allocator operating over a contiguous
//! physical memory slice or pointer without relying on OS virtual memory or libc malloc.

#[cfg(not(feature = "std"))]
use alloc::vec::Vec;
use core::ptr::NonNull;

#[derive(Debug, PartialEq)]
pub enum MemoryError {
    OutOfMemory { requested: usize, available: usize },
    InvalidAlignment,
    NullPointer,
}

/// A contiguous, self-contained physical or virtual memory region.
/// Passed by an exokernel (like BareMetal OS), bootloader, or host substrate.
pub struct MemoryRegion {
    pub base: NonNull<u8>,
    pub size: usize,
}

impl MemoryRegion {
    /// Create a memory region from a raw pointer and size.
    ///
    /// # Safety
    /// The caller must ensure that `base` points to at least `size` bytes of valid, writable memory.
    pub unsafe fn from_raw(base: *mut u8, size: usize) -> Result<Self, MemoryError> {
        let ptr = NonNull::new(base).ok_or(MemoryError::NullPointer)?;
        Ok(Self { base: ptr, size })
    }

    /// Create a memory region borrowing a mutable byte slice.
    pub fn from_slice(slice: &mut [u8]) -> Self {
        let ptr = NonNull::new(slice.as_mut_ptr()).expect("Slice pointer cannot be null");
        Self {
            base: ptr,
            size: slice.len(),
        }
    }
}

/// A deterministic, self-contained Bump Allocator operating entirely within a `MemoryRegion`.
pub struct BumpArena {
    base: NonNull<u8>,
    capacity: usize,
    allocated: usize,
}

impl BumpArena {
    pub fn new(region: MemoryRegion) -> Self {
        Self {
            base: region.base,
            capacity: region.size,
            allocated: 0,
        }
    }

    /// Allocate `size` bytes aligned to `align` bytes.
    pub fn alloc(&mut self, size: usize, align: usize) -> Result<NonNull<u8>, MemoryError> {
        if !align.is_power_of_two() {
            return Err(MemoryError::InvalidAlignment);
        }

        let current_ptr = unsafe { self.base.as_ptr().add(self.allocated) as usize };
        let aligned_ptr = (current_ptr + align - 1) & !(align - 1);
        let padding = aligned_ptr - current_ptr;

        let total_needed = padding.checked_add(size).ok_or(MemoryError::OutOfMemory {
            requested: size,
            available: self.capacity.saturating_sub(self.allocated),
        })?;

        if self.allocated + total_needed > self.capacity {
            return Err(MemoryError::OutOfMemory {
                requested: size,
                available: self.capacity.saturating_sub(self.allocated),
            });
        }

        self.allocated += total_needed;
        NonNull::new(aligned_ptr as *mut u8).ok_or(MemoryError::NullPointer)
    }

    /// Reset the arena to zero allocations without releasing the underlying memory region.
    pub fn reset(&mut self) {
        self.allocated = 0;
    }

    pub fn used(&self) -> usize {
        self.allocated
    }

    pub fn available(&self) -> usize {
        self.capacity.saturating_sub(self.allocated)
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn test_bump_arena_allocation_and_alignment() {
        let mut buffer = [0u8; 1024];
        let region = MemoryRegion::from_slice(&mut buffer);
        let mut arena = BumpArena::new(region);

        // Allocate 10 bytes with 8-byte alignment
        let p1 = arena.alloc(10, 8).expect("Allocation 1 failed");
        assert_eq!(p1.as_ptr() as usize % 8, 0);

        // Allocate 5 bytes with 16-byte alignment
        let p2 = arena.alloc(5, 16).expect("Allocation 2 failed");
        assert_eq!(p2.as_ptr() as usize % 16, 0);

        assert!(arena.used() > 15);
        assert!(arena.available() < 1024);

        // Reset
        arena.reset();
        assert_eq!(arena.used(), 0);
        assert_eq!(arena.available(), 1024);
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_bump_arena_out_of_memory() {
        let mut buffer = [0u8; 64];
        let region = MemoryRegion::from_slice(&mut buffer);
        let mut arena = BumpArena::new(region);

        let res = arena.alloc(128, 8);
        assert_eq!(
            res,
            Err(MemoryError::OutOfMemory {
                requested: 128,
                available: 64
            })
        );
    }
}

//! Cell data model and value representations.

#[cfg(not(feature = "std"))]
use alloc::{boxed::Box, format, string::String, vec::Vec};

use super::coord::Coord;

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub enum CellValue {
    Empty,
    Number(f64),
    Text(String),
    Boolean(bool),
    Formula {
        expr: String,
        cached_num: Option<f64>,
        cached_text: Option<String>,
        cached_bool: Option<bool>,
    },
    Basic {
        source: String,
    },
    Blob {
        bytes: Vec<u8>,
    },
    Error(String),
}

impl CellValue {
    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, CellValue::Empty)
    }

    /// Interpret text the way typing it into a cell does: `=...` is a formula,
    /// numbers and TRUE/FALSE are recognised, blank input is empty, anything
    /// else is text. Typing, pasting and filling all go through this.
    pub fn parse_input(text: &str) -> CellValue {
        let text = text.trim();
        if text.is_empty() {
            CellValue::Empty
        } else if text.starts_with('=') {
            CellValue::Formula {
                expr: text.into(),
                cached_num: None,
                cached_text: None,
                cached_bool: None,
            }
        } else if let Ok(n) = text.parse::<f64>() {
            CellValue::Number(n)
        } else if text.eq_ignore_ascii_case("true") {
            CellValue::Boolean(true)
        } else if text.eq_ignore_ascii_case("false") {
            CellValue::Boolean(false)
        } else {
            CellValue::Text(text.into())
        }
    }

    /// The text shown in a grid cell: numbers to two decimals, formulas by
    /// their last result, scripts and blobs as a tag.
    pub fn display_text(&self) -> String {
        match self {
            CellValue::Number(n) => format!("{:.2}", n),
            CellValue::Text(t) => t.clone(),
            CellValue::Formula { cached_num, cached_text, cached_bool, .. } => cached_num
                .map(|n| format!("{:.2}", n))
                .or_else(|| cached_bool.map(|b| if b { "TRUE".into() } else { "FALSE".into() }))
                .or_else(|| cached_text.clone())
                .unwrap_or_else(|| "<...>".into()),
            CellValue::Boolean(b) => (if *b { "TRUE" } else { "FALSE" }).into(),
            CellValue::Basic { .. } => "[BASIC]".into(),
            CellValue::Blob { .. } => "[BLOB]".into(),
            CellValue::Error(e) => format!("#ERR: {}", e),
            CellValue::Empty => String::new(),
        }
    }

    pub fn type_name(&self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            CellValue::Empty => "empty",
            CellValue::Number(_) => "number",
            CellValue::Text(_) => "text",
            CellValue::Boolean(_) => "boolean",
            CellValue::Formula { .. } => "formula",
            CellValue::Basic { .. } => "basic",
            CellValue::Blob { .. } => "blob",
            CellValue::Error(_) => "error",
        }
    }
}

/// A discrete unit in the spreadsheet grid.
///
/// In Lotus 5-7-11:
/// - A cell can hold numbers, text, and formulas.
/// - A cell can be a **named blob** holding arbitrary raw file bytes.
/// - A cell can hold embedded BASIC source code.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct Cell {
    pub coord: Coord,
    pub id: Option<String>,
    pub name: Option<String>,
    /// Explicit workbook-cell access granted to this embedded BASIC procedure.
    pub basic_reads: Vec<String>,
    pub basic_writes: Vec<String>,
    /// Explicit links to embedded documents, optionally with a `#section` fragment.
    pub document_refs: Vec<String>,
    /// Flag indicating this cell stores a binary/media/vector asset rather than active spreadsheet grid data.
    pub is_asset: bool,
    /// Column span in the logical point grid (default: 1).
    pub col_span: u32,
    /// Row span in the logical point grid (default: 1).
    pub row_span: u32,
    /// Optional reference to a vector blob cell (curtain overlay projection).
    pub overlay: Option<String>,
    pub value: CellValue,
}

impl Cell {
    pub fn new(coord: Coord, value: CellValue) -> Self {
        let is_asset = coord.row >= 10_000;
        Self {
            coord,
            id: None,
            name: None,
            basic_reads: Vec::new(),
            basic_writes: Vec::new(),
            document_refs: Vec::new(),
            is_asset,
            col_span: 1,
            row_span: 1,
            overlay: None,
            value,
        }
    }

    pub fn with_span(mut self, col_span: u32, row_span: u32) -> Self {
        self.col_span = col_span.max(1);
        self.row_span = row_span.max(1);
        self
    }

    pub fn with_overlay(mut self, overlay: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        self.overlay = Some(overlay.into());
        self
    }

    pub fn with_asset(mut self, is_asset: bool) -> Self {
        self.is_asset = is_asset;
        self
    }

    pub fn is_asset(&self) -> bool {
        self.is_asset || self.coord.row >= 10_000
    }

    pub fn with_name(mut self, name: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        self.name = Some(name.into());
        self
    }

    pub fn with_id(mut self, id: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        self.id = Some(id.into());
        self
    }

    pub fn with_basic_grants(mut self, reads: Vec<String>, writes: Vec<String>) -> Self {
        self.basic_reads = reads;
        self.basic_writes = writes;
        self
    }

    pub fn with_document_refs(mut self, document_refs: Vec<String>) -> Self {
        self.document_refs = document_refs;
        self
    }

    pub fn number(coord: Coord, val: f64) -> Self {
        Self::new(coord, CellValue::Number(val))
    }

    pub fn text(coord: Coord, val: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        Self::new(coord, CellValue::Text(val.into()))
    }

    pub fn boolean(coord: Coord, val: bool) -> Self {
        Self::new(coord, CellValue::Boolean(val))
    }

    pub fn formula(coord: Coord, expr: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        Self::new(
            coord,
            CellValue::Formula {
                expr: expr.into(),
                cached_num: None,
                cached_text: None,
                cached_bool: None,
            },
        )
    }

    pub fn basic(coord: Coord, name: impl Into<String>, source: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        Self::new(coord, CellValue::Basic { source: source.into() }).with_name(name)
    }

    pub fn blob(coord: Coord, name: impl Into<String>, bytes: Vec<u8>) -> Self {
        Self::new(coord, CellValue::Blob { bytes }).with_name(name)
    }


}

//! Spreadsheet grid coordinate system (0-indexed internally, 1-indexed A1 externally).

#[cfg(not(feature = "std"))]
use alloc::string::String;
use core::fmt;

/// Spatial coordinate on a worksheet grid.
/// `col` is 0-indexed (0 = A, 1 = B, 26 = AA, etc.).
/// `row` is 0-indexed (0 = 1, 26 = 27, etc.).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash)]
pub struct Coord {
    pub col: u32,
    pub row: u32,
}

impl Coord {
    pub const fn new(col: u32, row: u32) -> Self {
        Self { col, row }
    }

    /// Parse an A1-notation reference (e.g. "A1", "D27", "AA100") into a `Coord`.
    pub fn parse_a1(s: &str) -> Option<Self> {
        let s = s.trim();
        if s.is_empty() {
            return None;
        }

        let mut col: u32 = 0;
        let mut row_start = 0;
        let mut has_letters = false;

        for (i, b) in s.bytes().enumerate() {
            if b.is_ascii_alphabetic() {
                has_letters = true;
                let val = (b.to_ascii_uppercase() - b'A' + 1) as u32;
                col = col.checked_mul(26)?.checked_add(val)?;
            } else if b.is_ascii_digit() {
                row_start = i;
                break;
            } else {
                return None;
            }
        }

        if !has_letters || row_start == 0 {
            return None;
        }

        let row_str = &s[row_start..];
        let row_1based: u32 = row_str.parse().ok()?;
        if row_1based == 0 {
            return None;
        }

        Some(Self {
            col: col - 1,
            row: row_1based - 1,
        })
    }

    /// Format this coordinate into standard A1-notation (e.g. `Coord { col: 3, row: 26 }` -> `"D27"`).
    pub fn to_a1(&self) -> String {
        let mut col_str = String::new();
        let mut c = self.col + 1;
        while c > 0 {
            let rem = ((c - 1) % 26) as u8;
            col_str.insert(0, (b'A' + rem) as char);
            c = (c - 1) / 26;
        }

        #[cfg(feature = "std")]
        return format!("{}{}", col_str, self.row + 1);

        #[cfg(not(feature = "std"))]
        {
            use alloc::format;
            format!("{}{}", col_str, self.row + 1)
        }
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for Coord {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "{}", self.to_a1())
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn test_coord_a1_roundtrip() {
        let cases = [
            ("A1", Coord::new(0, 0)),
            ("B2", Coord::new(1, 1)),
            ("Z1", Coord::new(25, 0)),
            ("AA1", Coord::new(26, 0)),
            ("AB10", Coord::new(27, 9)),
            ("D27", Coord::new(3, 26)),
            ("ZZ999", Coord::new(701, 998)),
        ];

        for (a1, coord) in cases {
            assert_eq!(Coord::parse_a1(a1), Some(coord), "Failed parsing {}", a1);
            assert_eq!(coord.to_a1(), a1, "Failed formatting {:?}", coord);
        }
    }
}

//! Worksheet representation and cell collection.

#[cfg(not(feature = "std"))]
use alloc::{collections::BTreeMap, string::String, vec::Vec};
#[cfg(feature = "std")]
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

use super::{cell::Cell, coord::Coord, vector::Rect2D};

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct Sheet {
    pub id: String,
    pub name: String,
    pub cells: BTreeMap<Coord, Cell>,
    /// Column widths in logical vector units (overrides default).
    pub col_widths: BTreeMap<u32, f32>,
    /// Row heights in logical vector units (overrides default).
    pub row_heights: BTreeMap<u32, f32>,
    pub default_col_width: f32,
    pub default_row_height: f32,
}

impl Sheet {
    pub fn new(id: impl Into<String>, name: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        Self {
            id: id.into(),
            name: name.into(),
            cells: BTreeMap::new(),
            col_widths: BTreeMap::new(),
            row_heights: BTreeMap::new(),
            default_col_width: 100.0,
            default_row_height: 24.0,
        }
    }

    /// Insert or update a cell in the sheet.
    pub fn set_cell(&mut self, cell: Cell) {
        self.cells.insert(cell.coord, cell);
    }

    /// Retrieve a reference to a cell at a specific coordinate.
    pub fn get_cell(&self, coord: Coord) -> Option<&Cell> {
        self.cells.get(&coord)
    }

    /// Retrieve a mutable reference to a cell at a specific coordinate.
    pub fn get_cell_mut(&mut self, coord: Coord) -> Option<&mut Cell> {
        self.cells.get_mut(&coord)
    }

    /// Calculate the furthest used column and row in this sheet.
    /// Returns `None` if the sheet has no cells.
    pub fn bounds(&self) -> Option<(u32, u32)> {
        if self.cells.is_empty() {
            return None;
        }

        let mut max_col = 0;
        let mut max_row = 0;
        for coord in self.cells.keys() {
            if coord.col > max_col {
                max_col = coord.col;
            }
            if coord.row > max_row {
                max_row = coord.row;
            }
        }

        Some((max_col, max_row))
    }

    /// Calculate the furthest used column and row for active spreadsheet cells,
    /// explicitly excluding far-out binary/media/vector asset rows so that the
    /// Dynamic Table of Contents (TOC) remains anchored directly adjacent to the
    /// visible computational grid.
    pub fn active_grid_bounds(&self) -> Option<(u32, u32)> {
        let mut max_col = 0;
        let mut max_row = 0;
        let mut found = false;

        for cell in self.cells.values() {
            if cell.is_asset() {
                continue;
            }
            found = true;
            if cell.coord.col > max_col {
                max_col = cell.coord.col;
            }
            if cell.coord.row > max_row {
                max_row = cell.coord.row;
            }
        }

        if found {
            Some((max_col, max_row))
        } else {
            None
        }
    }

    /// Retrieve the logical vector width of a column.
    pub fn col_width(&self, col: u32) -> f32 {
        self.col_widths.get(&col).copied().unwrap_or(self.default_col_width)
    }

    /// Set a custom width for a column in logical vector units.
    pub fn set_col_width(&mut self, col: u32, width: f32) {
        self.col_widths.insert(col, width.max(10.0));
    }

    /// Retrieve the logical vector height of a row.
    pub fn row_height(&self, row: u32) -> f32 {
        self.row_heights.get(&row).copied().unwrap_or(self.default_row_height)
    }

    /// Set a custom height for a row in logical vector units.
    pub fn set_row_height(&mut self, row: u32, height: f32) {
        self.row_heights.insert(row, height.max(8.0));
    }

    /// Compute the logical 2D bounding rectangle of a cell, taking into account
    /// custom column widths, custom row heights, and `col_span` / `row_span`.
    pub fn cell_rect(&self, coord: Coord) -> Rect2D {
        // Calculate X offset by summing previous column widths
        let mut min_x = 0.0f32;
        for c in 0..coord.col {
            min_x += self.col_width(c);
        }

        // Calculate Y offset by summing previous row heights
        let mut min_y = 0.0f32;
        for r in 0..coord.row {
            min_y += self.row_height(r);
        }

        let (col_span, row_span) = self
            .get_cell(coord)
            .map(|cell| (cell.col_span, cell.row_span))
            .unwrap_or((1, 1));

        let mut span_width = 0.0f32;
        for c in coord.col..(coord.col + col_span) {
            span_width += self.col_width(c);
        }

        let mut span_height = 0.0f32;
        for r in coord.row..(coord.row + row_span) {
            span_height += self.row_height(r);
        }

        Rect2D::new(min_x, min_y, min_x + span_width, min_y + span_height)
    }

    /// Determines if an overlapping cell (either a Large Cell with span > 1x1 or
    /// a Curtain overlay) is concealing/obfuscating underlying cells that contain data.
    ///
    /// When true, the user interface renders a thin red line at the bottom of the
    /// anchor cell to inform the user that calculation data is tucked underneath.
    pub fn cell_obfuscates_data(&self, coord: Coord) -> bool {
        let cell = match self.get_cell(coord) {
            Some(c) => c,
            None => return false,
        };

        let has_span = cell.col_span > 1 || cell.row_span > 1;
        let has_overlay = cell.overlay.is_some();

        if !has_span && !has_overlay {
            return false;
        }

        // Inspect all covered coordinates in the span, skipping the anchor cell itself
        for c in coord.col..(coord.col + cell.col_span) {
            for r in coord.row..(coord.row + cell.row_span) {
                if c == coord.col && r == coord.row {
                    continue;
                }
                let covered_coord = Coord::new(c, r);
                if let Some(covered_cell) = self.get_cell(covered_coord) {
                    if !covered_cell.value.is_empty() {
                        return true;
                    }
                }
            }
        }

        false
    }
}

//! Dynamic Table of Contents (TOC) positioned at (maxX + 1, maxY + 1).

#[cfg(not(feature = "std"))]
use alloc::{string::String, vec::Vec};

use super::coord::Coord;

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct TocEntry {
    pub sheet_id: String,
    pub coord: Coord,
    pub name: String,
    pub type_name: String,
    pub byte_size: usize,
}

/// The Dynamic Table of Contents.
///
/// In Lotus 5-7-11, the TOC is not at a static location.
/// It dynamically sits one cell beyond the furthest used column and row:
/// `pos = (maxX + 1, maxY + 1)`.
/// E.g., if used cells are within columns A..C (0..2) and rows 1..26 (0..25),
/// the TOC resides at D27 (col 3, row 26).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct TableOfContents {
    pub pos: Coord,
    pub extent_col: u32,
    pub extent_row: u32,
    pub entries: Vec<TocEntry>,
}
